📘 How to Use This Material

  1. Each topic mixes several distinct question formats — don't assume every question follows the same pattern.
  2. Read the Quick Shortcuts box before starting a topic — use it in the exam, not long-form working.
  3. Click a question to reveal the step-by-step solution — try solving it yourself first.
  4. This document prints cleanly — use your browser's Print to get a PDF with all answers visible.
What's new in this edition: Added 5 new topics — Classification (Odd One Out), Numeric Analogy, Coded Inequality, Syllogism, and Linear Seating Arrangement. Every existing topic now mixes 2-4 distinct question formats instead of one repeated template (e.g. Number Series now includes AP, GP, squares, cubes, alternating, growing-difference, wrong-term, and missing-term variants). Non-verbal figure-based topics (mirror/paper folding images, embedded figures) still need visual diagrams and are not yet included.

TOPIC 1

Number Series

120 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Check differences, then ratios, then squares/cubes, then check if differences themselves grow.
  • For 'find the wrong term' questions, first establish the rule from 4-5 clearly consistent terms.
  • Alternating series often hide two interleaved simpler series — split odd/even positions.
Q1Find the next number in the series: 9, 16, 23, 30, 37, ?
  1. Each term increases by a constant difference of 7.
  2. Next term = 37 + 7 = 44.
Answer: 44
Q2Find the next number in the series: 5, 10, 20, 40, 80, ?
  1. Each term is multiplied by a constant ratio of 2.
  2. Next term = 80 × 2 = 160.
Answer: 160
Q3Find the next number in the series: 81, 100, 121, 144, 169, ?
  1. The terms are consecutive perfect squares starting from 9².
Answer: 196
Q4Find the next number in the series: 1331, 1728, 2197, 2744, 3375, ?
  1. The terms are consecutive perfect cubes starting from 11³.
Answer: 4096
Q5Find the next number in the series: 29, 38, 32, 41, 35, ?
  1. The series alternately adds 9 and subtracts 6.
Answer: 29
Q6Find the next number in the series: 9, 11, 15, 21, 29, ?
  1. The differences between terms increase by 2 each time.
  2. Next difference = 10, so next term = 29 + 10 = 39.
Answer: 39
Q7Find the wrong number in the series: 2, 4, 8, 11, 14, 17
  1. The correct series should increase by a constant 3 each step.
  2. Term at position 2 should be 5, not 4.
Answer: 4
Q8Find the missing number in the series: 8, ?, 26, 35, 44, 53
  1. The series increases by a constant 9 each step.
  2. Missing term = 8 + 9 = 17.
Answer: 17

TOPIC 2

Alphabet Series

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Convert letters to position numbers (A=1...Z=26) before spotting the pattern.
  • Paired-letter series often move the two halves in opposite directions.
  • Wrap around using mod 26 when the position exceeds 26.
Q1Find the next letter in the series: L, N, P, R, T, ?
  1. Letter positions increase by 2 each step.
  2. Next position = 22 → letter V.
Answer: V
Q2Find the next letter in the series: Y, V, S, P, M, ?
  1. Letter positions decrease by 3 each step.
  2. Next position = 10 → letter J.
Answer: J
Q3Find the next group of letters: AZ, BY, CX, DW, ?
  1. First letter of each pair moves forward (+1); second letter moves backward (−1) from Z.
  2. Next pair = EV.
Answer: EV
Q4Find the wrong letter in the series: Q, T, W, X, C, F
  1. Letter positions should increase by 3 each step.
  2. Position 4 should be Z, not X.
Answer: X
Q5Find the next letter in the series: J, M, P, S, V, ?
  1. Letter positions increase by 3 each step.
  2. Next position = 25 → letter Y.
Answer: Y
Q6Find the next letter in the series: Q, O, M, K, I, ?
  1. Letter positions decrease by 2 each step.
  2. Next position = 7 → letter G.
Answer: G
Q7Find the next group of letters: CX, DW, EV, FU, ?
  1. First letter of each pair moves forward (+1); second letter moves backward (−1) from Z.
  2. Next pair = GT.
Answer: GT
Q8Find the wrong letter in the series: C, E, G, I, L, M
  1. Letter positions should increase by 2 each step.
  2. Position 5 should be K, not L.
Answer: L
Q9Find the next letter in the series: N, O, P, Q, R, ?
  1. Letter positions increase by 1 each step.
  2. Next position = 19 → letter S.
Answer: S
Q10Find the next letter in the series: O, N, M, L, K, ?
  1. Letter positions decrease by 1 each step.
  2. Next position = 10 → letter J.
Answer: J
Q11Find the next group of letters: BY, CX, DW, EV, ?
  1. First letter of each pair moves forward (+1); second letter moves backward (−1) from Z.
  2. Next pair = FU.
Answer: FU

  1. Letter positions should increase by 3 each step.

Answer: B

TOPIC 3

Coding-Decoding

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • For shift ciphers, find k from just the first letter, then apply it to the rest.
  • For word-code mapping questions, find the ONE word common to both given groups first.
  • Write the alphabet with position numbers as a quick reference during the exam.
Q1In a certain code, each letter is shifted forward by 6 positions in the alphabet (A→G). How is the word 'MARKET' written in this code?
  1. Shift every letter of MARKET forward by 6 positions (wrap Z→A as needed).
Answer: SGXQKZ
Q2In a certain code language, 'RIVER HORSE TABLE' is written as 'YXJ XVY VYV' and 'TABLE GLASS TIGER' is written as 'YKK XXX VYV'. What is the code for 'TABLE'?
  1. 'TABLE' is the only word common to both groups.
  2. The code common to both coded groups must represent 'TABLE'.
  3. That common code is 'VYV'.
Answer: VYV
Q3In a certain code, each letter is shifted forward by 6 positions in the alphabet (A→G). How is the word 'FLOWER' written in this code?
  1. Shift every letter of FLOWER forward by 6 positions (wrap Z→A as needed).
Answer: LRUCKX

  2. The code common to both coded groups must represent 'BREAD'.
  3. That common code is 'WWJ'.
Answer: WWJ

TOPIC 4

Blood Relations

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Draw a simple family tree with '+' for male and '−' for female as you read.
  • Solve chain relations one step at a time — never combine both steps mentally.
  • For 'pointing to a photograph' puzzles, identify the speaker's gender first — it often decides the answer.
Q1Wasim said, 'Chetan is my father's only brother.' How is Chetan related to Wasim?
  1. Break the relation into simple steps and draw a mini family tree.
  2. Working through the described relation gives: Uncle.
Answer: Uncle
Q2Zara's father is Tarun. Tarun's brother is Chirag. How is Chirag related to Zara?
  1. Zara's father is Tarun, and Tarun's brother is Chirag.
  2. Combine the two relations: father + brother = uncle.
Answer: uncle
Q3Priya's husband is Aman. Aman's sister is Chirag. How is Chirag related to Priya?
  1. Priya's husband is Aman, and Aman's sister is Chirag.
  2. Combine the two relations: husband + sister = sister-in-law.
Answer: sister-in-law
Q4Anil's sister is Priya. Priya's son is Tarun. How is Tarun related to Anil?
  1. Anil's sister is Priya, and Priya's son is Tarun.
  2. Combine the two relations: sister + son = nephew.
Answer: nephew
Q5Sanya's father is Wasim. Wasim's sister is Tarun. How is Tarun related to Sanya?
  1. Sanya's father is Wasim, and Wasim's sister is Tarun.
  2. Combine the two relations: father + sister = aunt.
Answer: aunt

  2. Working through the described relation gives: Daughter.
Answer: Daughter

TOPIC 5

Direction Sense

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Plot movements on an X-Y grid: North/South on Y-axis, East/West on X-axis.
  • For perpendicular legs, use the Pythagorean theorem directly for shortest distance.
  • Right turn = clockwise rotation; left turn = anticlockwise rotation.
Q1A person walks 8 km towards North, then turns and walks 15 km towards East. What is the shortest distance from the starting point?
  1. The two legs (8 km, 15 km) are perpendicular.
  2. Distance = √(8²+15²) = √289 = 17 km.
Answer: 17 km
Q2A person is facing West. If they turn 90° to their left, which direction are they facing now?
  1. Turning right rotates the facing direction clockwise; turning left rotates it anticlockwise.
  2. From West, a 90° left turn faces South.
Answer: South
Q3A person walks 3 km towards West, then 4 km towards North. In which direction is he now from the starting point?
  1. Moving 3 km West then 4 km North places him diagonally from the start.
  2. That combined direction is North-West.
Answer: North-West
Q4A person walks 7 km towards North, then turns and walks 24 km towards East. What is the shortest distance from the starting point?
  1. The two legs (7 km, 24 km) are perpendicular.
  2. Distance = √(7²+24²) = √625 = 25 km.
Answer: 25 km
Q5A person is facing West. If they turn 90° to their right, which direction are they facing now?
  1. Turning right rotates the facing direction clockwise; turning left rotates it anticlockwise.
  2. From West, a 90° right turn faces North.
Answer: North
Q6A person walks 3 km towards West, then 4 km towards South. In which direction is he now from the starting point?
  1. Moving 3 km West then 4 km South places him diagonally from the start.
  2. That combined direction is South-West.
Answer: South-West

Answer: 20 km

  2. Distance = √(6²+8²) = √100 = 10 km.
Answer: 10 km

TOPIC 6

Ranking & Order

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Rank from bottom = Total − Rank from top + 1 (and vice versa).
  • Total = (Position from front) + (Position from back) − 1.
  • For 'people between', subtract 1 after finding the position gap.
Q1In a class of 28 students, Kavya ranks 25th from the top. What is Kavya's rank from the bottom?
  1. Rank from bottom = Total − Rank from top + 1 (and vice versa).
  2. = 28 − 25 + 1 = 4.
Answer: 4
Q2In a queue, Kavya is 22nd from the front and 13th from the back. How many people are in the queue?
  1. Total = (Position from front) + (Position from back) − 1.
  2. = 22 + 13 − 1 = 34.
Answer: 34
Q3In a row of 54 people, Qasim is at position 28 from the left and Anil is at position 30 from the left. How many people are seated between them?
  1. Number between = (larger position − smaller position − 1).
  2. = 30 − 28 − 1 = 1.
Answer: 1
Q4In a class of 46 students, Gita ranks 46th from the bottom. What is Gita's rank from the top?
  1. Rank from bottom = Total − Rank from top + 1 (and vice versa).
  2. = 46 − 46 + 1 = 1.
Answer: 1
Q5In a queue, Lokesh is 5th from the front and 25th from the back. How many people are in the queue?
  1. Total = (Position from front) + (Position from back) − 1.
  2. = 5 + 25 − 1 = 29.
Answer: 29
Q6In a row of 42 people, Lokesh is at position 6 from the left and Priya is at position 9 from the left. How many people are seated between them?
  1. Number between = (larger position − smaller position − 1).
  2. = 9 − 6 − 1 = 2.
Answer: 2
Q7In a class of 43 students, Nikhil ranks 2nd from the bottom. What is Nikhil's rank from the top?

  2. = 43 − 2 + 1 = 42.
Answer: 42

TOPIC 7

⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Test the same simple operation across all given rows/columns first.
  • Once a rule fits two full rows/columns perfectly, apply it to the incomplete one.
  • If row-wise fails, always try column-wise before giving up.

TOPIC 8

Mirror & Water Image (Clock)

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Mirror image: subtract time from 11:60.
  • Water image: subtract time from 6:60.
  • These two formulas are different — don't mix them up under exam pressure.
Q1A clock shows the time as 5:00. What time will it show in its mirror image?
  1. Mirror image formula: subtract the time from 11:60.
  2. 11:60 − 5:00 = 7:00.
Answer: 7:00
Q2A clock shows the time as 11:40. What time will it show in its water (vertical mirror) image?
  1. Water image formula: subtract the time from 6:60.
  2. 6:60 − 11:40 = 6:20.
Answer: 6:20
Q3A clock shows the time as 2:40. What time will it show in its mirror image?
  1. Mirror image formula: subtract the time from 11:60.
  2. 11:60 − 2:40 = 9:20.
Answer: 9:20

  2. 6:60 − 7:45 = 10:15.
Answer: 10:15

TOPIC 9

Calendar (Day of Week)

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • The day-of-week cycle repeats every 7 days — reduce day counts using mod 7.
  • A common year has 1 odd day; a leap year has 2 — this shifts the same date's weekday.
  • Counting forward moves later in the week; counting backward wraps around.
Q1If 1st January of a year is a Friday (non-leap year), what day of the week will 1st January be 2 year(s) later, assuming no leap year falls in between?
  1. A common (non-leap) year has 1 'odd day', so the day of the week shifts forward by 1 for each such year.
  2. After 2 year(s), the day shifts forward by 2 → Sunday.
Answer: Sunday
Q2If 1st January of a year is a Monday (non-leap year), what day of the week will 1st January be 1 year(s) later, assuming no leap year falls in between?
  1. A common (non-leap) year has 1 'odd day', so the day of the week shifts forward by 1 for each such year.
  2. After 1 year(s), the day shifts forward by 1 → Tuesday.
Answer: Tuesday
Q3If today is Thursday, what day of the week was it 69 days before today?
  1. 69 mod 7 = 6 (the weekly cycle repeats every 7 days).
  2. Count 6 day(s) before from Thursday.
Answer: Friday
Q4If 1st January of a year is a Thursday (non-leap year), what day of the week will 1st January be 1 year(s) later, assuming no leap year falls in between?
  1. A common (non-leap) year has 1 'odd day', so the day of the week shifts forward by 1 for each such year.
  2. After 1 year(s), the day shifts forward by 1 → Friday.
Answer: Friday

  1. 22 mod 7 = 1 (the weekly cycle repeats every 7 days).
  2. Count 1 day(s) before from Wednesday.
Answer: Tuesday

  1. 19 mod 7 = 5 (the weekly cycle repeats every 7 days).
  2. Count 5 day(s) before from Friday.
Answer: Sunday

Q90If 1st January of a year is a Saturday (non-leap year), what day of the week will 1st January be 2 year(s) later, assuming no leap year falls in between?
  1. A common (non-leap) year has 1 'odd day', so the day of the week shifts forward by 1 for each such year.
  2. After 2 year(s), the day shifts forward by 2 → Monday.
Answer: Monday
Q91If today is Thursday, what day of the week will it be 30 days after today?
  1. 30 mod 7 = 2 (the weekly cycle repeats every 7 days).
  2. Count 2 day(s) after from Thursday.

  2. After 1 year(s), the day shifts forward by 1 → Monday.

TOPIC 10

Dictionary / Alphabetical Order

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Compare words letter by letter from the left, exactly like a real dictionary.
  • If first letters match, move to the second letter, and so on.
  • List all given words in a column first, then sort — don't sort purely mentally.
Q1If the words NIGHT, GARDEN, CANDLE, FLAME, ZEBRA are arranged in dictionary (alphabetical) order, which word comes 2nd?
  1. Arrange alphabetically: CANDLE, FLAME, GARDEN, NIGHT, ZEBRA.
  2. The 2nd word is 'FLAME'.
Answer: FLAME
Q2If the words OCEAN, VELVET, YELLOW, PENCIL, SILVER are arranged in dictionary (alphabetical) order, which word comes 2nd?

  2. The 2nd word is 'PENCIL'.

TOPIC 11

Classification (Odd One Out)

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • First scan for the obvious pattern: even/odd, multiples, squares, or primes.
  • Test each number against your hypothesized rule before finalizing the odd one.
  • If numbers don't share an arithmetic property, check digit sums or digit patterns.

⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Identify the exact operation linking the first pair before applying it to the second.
  • Common relations: square, cube, double/triple, add a constant, digit sum.
  • Verify your rule works cleanly on the given pair before applying it forward.

TOPIC 13

Coded Inequality

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Convert each symbol to plain '>', '<', or '=' first, mentally or on paper.
  • A chain only gives a definite answer when the directions are consistent throughout.
  • '=' combined with '>' or '<' still gives a definite result — treat it like a pass-through.
Q1If 'P@Q' means P is greater than Q, 'P#Q' means P is smaller than Q, and 'P$Q' means P is equal to Q, then from the statement 'E@F$B', what is the relationship between E and B?
  1. 'E@F' means E is greater than F.
  2. 'F$B' means F is equal to B.
  3. Combining both: E is greater than B.
Answer: E is greater than B
Q2If 'P@Q' means P is greater than Q, 'P#Q' means P is smaller than Q, and 'P$Q' means P is equal to Q, then from the statement 'A#C$B', what is the relationship between A and B?
  1. 'A#C' means A is smaller than C.
  2. 'C$B' means C is equal to B.
  3. Combining both: A is smaller than B.
Answer: A is smaller than B
Q3If 'P@Q' means P is greater than Q, 'P#Q' means P is smaller than Q, and 'P$Q' means P is equal to Q, then from the statement 'E#B$H', what is the relationship between E and H?
  1. 'E#B' means E is smaller than B.
  2. 'B$H' means B is equal to H.
  3. Combining both: E is smaller than H.
Answer: E is smaller than H
Q4If 'P@Q' means P is greater than Q, 'P#Q' means P is smaller than Q, and 'P$Q' means P is equal to Q, then from the statement 'H$A#F', what is the relationship between H and F?
  1. 'H$A' means H is equal to A.
  2. 'A#F' means A is smaller than F.
  3. Combining both: H is smaller than F.
Answer: H is smaller than F
Q5If 'P@Q' means P is greater than Q, 'P#Q' means P is smaller than Q, and 'P$Q' means P is equal to Q, then from the statement 'F$D#G', what is the relationship between F and G?
  1. 'F$D' means F is equal to D.
  2. 'D#G' means D is smaller than G.
  3. Combining both: F is smaller than G.
Answer: F is smaller than G

  1. 'D@F' means D is greater than F.
  2. 'F$G' means F is equal to G.
  3. Combining both: D is greater than G.
Answer: D is greater than G

  2. 'C#B' means C is smaller than B.
  3. Combining both: F is smaller than B.
Answer: F is smaller than B
Q86If 'P@Q' means P is greater than Q, 'P#Q' means P is smaller than Q, and 'P$Q' means P is equal to Q, then from the statement 'B$G#C', what is the relationship between B and C?
  1. 'B$G' means B is equal to G.
  2. 'G#C' means G is smaller than C.
  3. Combining both: B is smaller than C.
Answer: B is smaller than C
Q87If 'P@Q' means P is greater than Q, 'P#Q' means P is smaller than Q, and 'P$Q' means P is equal to Q, then from the statement 'C$A#B', what is the relationship between C and B?
  1. 'C$A' means C is equal to A.
  2. 'A#B' means A is smaller than B.
  3. Combining both: C is smaller than B.
Answer: C is smaller than B
Q88If 'P@Q' means P is greater than Q, 'P#Q' means P is smaller than Q, and 'P$Q' means P is equal to Q, then from the statement 'C$E@D', what is the relationship between C and D?
  1. 'C$E' means C is equal to E.
  2. 'E@D' means E is greater than D.
  3. Combining both: C is greater than D.
Answer: C is greater than D

↑ Back to top
TOPIC 14

Syllogism

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Always draw Venn diagrams — never judge validity by 'common sense' alone.
  • 'All A are B + All B are C' always gives 'All A are C' — a safe, memorable rule.
  • 'Some...Some' statements almost never give a guaranteed 'Some...' conclusion — a classic trap.
Q1Statements: Some Trebons are Ossilo. All Ossilo are Estoval.
Conclusion: Some Trebons are Estoval.
Does the conclusion logically follow from the statements?
  1. Draw Venn circles for each statement and check whether the conclusion circle is forced by both.
  2. The conclusion is a valid, forced consequence of the two statements.
Answer: Conclusion follows
Q2Statements: All Plexar are Estoval. No Estoval are Vimtro.
Conclusion: No Plexar are Vimtro.
Does the conclusion logically follow from the statements?
  1. Draw Venn circles for each statement and check whether the conclusion circle is forced by both.
  2. The conclusion is a valid, forced consequence of the two statements.
Answer: Conclusion follows

Q11Statements: Some Fendars are Duskin. Some Duskin are Zorbs.

  1. Draw Venn circles for each statement — with 'Some...Some', multiple valid diagrams are possible.
  2. Since more than one diagram fits the statements, the conclusion is not guaranteed.
Answer: Conclusion does not necessarily follow

TOPIC 15

Seating Arrangement (Linear)

100 Questions
⚡ Quick Shortcuts
  • Draw the row as numbered boxes (1 to N) and fill in names as you read clues.
  • 'Immediate left/right' means adjacent seat, one position only.
  • Recount positions from the correct end — left and right are easy to reverse by mistake.
Q16 people — Bhavna, Gita, Chirag, Harish, Anil, Vikram — are sitting in a row facing North, in that exact left-to-right order. What is Gita's position from the left?
  1. Since the row order given is left-to-right: Bhavna, Gita, Chirag, Harish, Anil, Vikram.
  2. Gita is at position 2 from the left.
Answer: 2
Q25 people — Aman, Qasim, Priya, Uma, Ira — are sitting in a row facing North, in that exact left-to-right order. How many people are sitting between Aman and Ira?
  1. Row order (left to right): Aman, Qasim, Priya, Uma, Ira.
  2. People between position 1 and 5: 3.
Answer: 3
Q37 people — Zara, Chirag, Anil, Om, Sanya, Gita, Divya — are sitting in a row facing North, in that exact left-to-right order. Who is sitting at the rightmost end?
  1. Row order (left to right): Zara, Chirag, Anil, Om, Sanya, Gita, Divya.
  2. The rightmost person is Divya.
Answer: Divya

  2. The person immediately right of Tarun is Ira.
